HE  following  pages  contain  a bibliography  of  the  Paracelsus  Library  of

the  late  E.  Schubert,  M.D.  The  Library  contains  194  editions  of  the
writings  of  Paracelsus,  548  works  whose  contents  partly  or  chiefly  treat  of
Paracelsus,  descriptions  of  his  time  and  of  the  places  where  he  lived  and  worked,
publications  of  his  friends  and  of  his  opponents,  and  lastly  a selection  of  351
works  on  Alchemy.  The  Library  consists  of  the  most  complete  collection  ever
formed  of  the  literature  on  the  great  reformer  in  the  science  of  medicine,  and
is  richer  in  original  editions  of  Paracelsus  than  the  British  Museum,  which
contains  80  editions  less  than  Dr.  Schubert’s  collection.

The  arrangement  of  the  works  in  the  first  part  of  the  Catalogue  is  according
to  the  date  of  publication,  so  far  as  this  could  be  ascertained ; the  contents  of
the  second  and  third  parts  are  in  alphabetical  order,  under  the  names  of  the
authors.  To  facilitate  reference  to  special  subjects,  an  index  has  been  given  to
the  second  part  of  the  Catalogue.

The  former  owner  of  this  library,  the  late  Edward  Schubert,  M.D.,  was  born
in  1822  at  Lueneburg,  and  died  at  Frankfurt  am  Main  in  1892.  He  began  the
collection  of  the  works  of  Paracelsus  in  conjunction  with  his  friend  Dr.  K.
Sudhoff,  while  a student  at  the  University.

Pie  continued  his  collections  and  researches  during  the  whole  of  his  life,
leaving  as  the  fruit  of  his  labours  this  unique  library  of  1100  books,  manuscripts,
portraits,  and  tracts.  His  researches  were  undertaken  with  the  intention  of
publishing  his  views  with  respect  to  Paracelsus,  who  deserved,  as  he  thought,
the  place  of  honour  as  a physician  and  a philosopher  in  the  history  of  his  time.
The  quantity  and  unwieldy  character  of  the  materials  rendered  it  impossible
for  him  to  fully  accomplish  his  purpose,  an  indication  of  which  he  gave  in  his
published  work,  “ Paracelsus  Forschungen,”  written  in  collaboration  with
Dr.  K.  Sudhoff  (Hochdahl,  near  Duesseldorf).  The  publication  of  this  work
confirmed  his  reputation  as  one  of  the  greatest  authorities  on  the  subject.

Dr.  Schubert’s  manuscript  notes,  the  result  of  his  researches,  accompany
the  Library,  and  greatly  increase  its  value,  and  will  also  materially  assist  a
future  worker  in  the  same  field.

The  Library,  as  a whole,  is  now  offered  for  sale.  No  part  of  it  will  be
sold  separately,  with  the  exception  of  the  portion  on  Alchemy.

W.  Wesley  & Son  desire  written  offers  to  be  sent  to  their  office  before  the
end  of  March  next.  Offers  received  through  Agents  cannot  be  entertained.

LONDON :

28  ESSEX  ST.,  STRAND,
December , i8q3.

W.  WESLEY  AND  SON,

of  books  wanted.  With  regard  to  NATURAL  HISTORY  and  SCIENTIFIC  WORKS,  they
are  in  a position  to  comply  with  every  demand.  They  invite  students  of  any  special  branch  of
Natural  or  Physical  Science  to  send  their  addresses,  if  they  desire  to  receive  early  information  as  to
die  titles  and  prices  of  any  new  works  treating  of  their  special  subject.
